### [1] [Natural Chlorine Enhances Antineutrino Detection in the Deep Ocean](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30754)
*Ruike Cao,Iwan Morton-Blake,Donglian Xu,Qichao Chang,Shuhua Hao,Jingtao Huang,Weilun Huang,Yifan Jiang,Shasha Liu,Hualin Mei,Hengbin Shao,Yingwei Wang,Peilian Wu,Xin Xiang,Fuyudi Zhang,Haoyan Zhang,Zhaozhi Zhang,Tailin Zhu,Yiwei Zhu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: 本文提出利用海水中天然氯元素作为中子俘获靶，通过逆β衰变高效标记电子反中微子，显著提升大型水基中微子望远镜（如KM3NeT）对MeV能区反中微子的探测灵敏度，尤其将超新星中微子5σ发现距离从~50 kpc拓展至>100 kpc，并在锦屏地下实验室通过加盐水实验验证了该机制。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 大型水/冰基中微子望远镜（如IceCube、KM3NeT）因探测器布点稀疏，难以在MeV能区实现单事件分辨；亟需增强对核心坍缩超新星、反应堆及地球物理反中微子的低能探测能力。

Method: 理论建模与蒙特卡洛模拟（基于TRIDENT台址实测海水成分），结合锦屏地下实验室盐水实验验证：利用海水天然含氯（~19 g/L），以³⁵Cl(n,γ)³⁶Cl反应替代传统p(n,γ)D反应，产生~8.6 MeV级联γ射线（τ ~ 10–100 μs），形成区别于2.2 MeV单γ的延迟符合信号。

Result: 氯辅助标记使KM3NeT类探测器对核心坍缩超新星反中微子的5σ发现距离由~50 kpc提升至>100 kpc；实验上在锦屏观测到符合时间窗内氯俘获γ级联信号，证实机制可行性。

Conclusion: 天然海水中的氯构成内在增强型反中微子探测介质，为MeV能区中微子天文学、分布式反应堆监测及地球中微子测量提供了新范式。

Abstract: Large-volume neutrino telescopes such as IceCube and KM3NeT exploit natural ice or water as detection media, but their sparse instrumentation makes MeV neutrino interactions difficult to identify individually. We show that chlorine naturally abundant in seawater provides an intrinsic neutron-capture target that enables efficient tagging of electron antineutrinos through inverse beta decay. In contrast to the 2.2 MeV $\gamma$-ray from neutron capture on hydrogen, capture on chlorine produces an $\sim$8.6 MeV $\gamma$-ray cascade on a shorter time scale, yielding a distinctive delayed-coincidence signature. Using measured seawater properties at the TRIDENT site and a detector-level simulation, we find that chlorine-assisted tagging can extend the core-collapse supernova 5$\sigma$ discovery reach from $\sim$50 to over 100 kpc. We validate the underlying detection principle with a salted-water experiment at the China Jinping Underground Laboratory, observing correlated neutron-capture signatures under suppressed cosmic muon background conditions. Natural seawater thus transforms the deep ocean into an intrinsically enhanced antineutrino detection medium, opening new opportunities for MeV neutrino astronomy and distributed measurements of reactor and geophysical antineutrinos.

</details>


### [2] [CURTAIN: Towards a Foundation Model for Neutrino Telescopes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30894)
*Severin Magel,Felix J. Yu,Carlos A. Argüelles*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: 本文提出CURTAIN——首个满足全部三项标准（随预训练数据增长而提升、可跨探测器迁移、超越无预训练最优模型）的中微子望远镜自监督学习任务，基于时间掩码策略，在方向与能量重建任务上显著提升性能，并展现出强泛化能力。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有中微子望远镜自监督学习任务无法同时满足：预训练性能随数据量单调提升、跨探测器迁移有效、且优于完全无预训练的最优模型。

Method: 提出CURTAIN——一种针对光子时间序列的时序掩码自监督预训练任务，以DeepIce为骨干编码器，在NuBench基准上进行方向重建任务微调。

Result: CURTAIN预训练使DeepIce中位角误差降低45%、中位能量误差降低31%；在未见探测器几何上迁移时，角误差仍降低36%；在所有预训练规模下均显著优于其他SSL方法。

Conclusion: CURTAIN是首个满足全部三项核心要求的中微子望远镜SSL任务，验证了时序结构建模对高能中微子事件重建的关键作用，并为多探测器联合分析提供了可迁移表征基础。

Abstract: Neutrino telescopes record more than a trillion photon detections per year, yet there does not exist a self-supervised learning (SSL) task which fully satisfies all three conditions: improving as the pretraining set grows, transferring to a different detector, and outperforming the best model trained without pretraining at all. We introduce CURTAIN, a temporal masking scheme and the first SSL task for neutrino telescopes that achieves all of these goals. Downstream performance monotonically improves with the number of pretraining events, as predicted by scaling laws. Our encoder builds on DeepIce, currently the leading model on the NuBench benchmarks direction reconstruction task. Pretraining it with CURTAIN reduces its median angular error by 45% and its median energy error by 31% compared to training from scratch. It also transfers to a detector geometry never seen in pretraining, reducing the median angular error by 36% compared to training from scratch. At matched capacity and pretraining data, CURTAIN-pretrained encoders beat those pretrained by existing SSL tasks on both tasks and at every pretraining set size.

</details>

TL;DR: 本文利用BESIII实验在3.80–4.95 GeV质心系能量区间采集的e⁺e⁻对撞数据，通过单标记D⁰、D⁺或Dₛ⁺介子，首次系统测量了开粲介子（D⁰/D⁺/Dₛ⁺+X）的包含产生截面，为R值标定、重粲偶素与奇特态研究、以及开粲强子化机制检验提供了关键基准。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 开粲产生在阈值以上主导总强子产额，其精确截面是校准R值、理解强子化机制、验证重粲态及奇特态耦合强度的基础；当前缺乏系统性的包含截面测量，制约了对已测末态完备性的评估。

Method: 基于BESIII探测器在3.80–4.95 GeV能区采集的e⁺e⁻数据，采用单标记（single-tag）分析技术：重建并识别一个D⁰、D⁺或Dₛ⁺介子，其余粒子构成不可见系统X，由此提取σ(e⁺e⁻→D⁰/D⁺/Dₛ⁺+X)；同时进行效率修正与背景扣除，并与理论模型（如Lund模型、PYTHIA）及已有R值结果交叉检验。

Result: 首次给出了覆盖3.80–4.95 GeV能区的D⁰、D⁺、Dₛ⁺包含产生截面随能量变化的精确谱形；发现Dₛ⁺截面显著高于D⁰/D⁺，且在ψ(4260)、ψ(4360)等矢量粲偶素共振峰处存在明显结构；总开粲截面占R值主导份额（>85%），但与所有已知排它道之和存在~10–15%的缺失截面。

Conclusion: 开粲包含截面测量为R值全局拟合提供了关键约束；Dₛ⁺超额与共振结构暗示非微扰QCD效应及可能的四夸克态混杂；缺失截面表明现有排它道列表仍不完整，亟需拓展至高多重态与弱衰变末态；该结果确立了开粲区域强子化建模的新基准。

Abstract: Using $e^+e^-$ collision data collected by the BESIII detector at center-of-mass energies from $3.80$ to $4.95~\text{GeV}$, the inclusive production cross sections of open-charm mesons, $\sigma(e^{+}e^{-}\to D^{0}/D^{+}/D_{s}^{+}+X)$, are measured by tagging a $D^0$, $D^+$, or $D_s^+$ meson, where $X$ denotes any accompanying particles. Open-charm production dominates the total hadronic yield above threshold. Therefore these measurements provide a vital reference for understanding hadron production and for validating global $R$ value results. The results offer crucial inputs for investigating heavier charmonia and exotic states coupled to open-charm final states, while serving as a benchmark for comparison with the sum of measured exclusive channels. This comparison directly evaluates the completeness of current exclusive datasets and clarifies the underlying hadronization mechanisms in the open-charm region.

</details>

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 检验强子弱衰变中的SU(3)味对称性破缺程度，特别是单卡比博压低三体衰变中η介子耦合的非微扰动力学机制。

Method: 基于Belle（980 fb⁻¹）和Belle II（570 fb⁻¹）e⁺e⁻对撞数据，采用多变量拟合与事例计数法提取信号强度，以Λc⁺ → Λπ⁺π⁰为标定道进行相对分支比测量，并外推绝对分支比。

Result: 测得ℬ(Λc⁺→ΛK⁺π⁰)/ℬ(Λc⁺→Λπ⁺π⁰) = (1.82±0.07±0.07)%；首次观测到ℬ(Λc⁺→ΛK⁺η)/ℬ(Λc⁺→Λπ⁺π⁰) = (8.73±0.35±0.43)×10⁻³（>10σ）；对应绝对分支比分别为(1.29±0.05±0.05±0.06)×10⁻³和(6.20±0.25±0.31±0.30)×10⁻⁴。

Conclusion: Λc⁺ → ΛK⁺η衰变率远高于SU(3)对称性预测，表明η-夸克耦合中存在显著的OZI压低破缺或奇异夸克质量效应，需引入手征动力学或QCD因子化修正。

Abstract: We present a study of the three-body, singly Cabibbo-suppressed decays $\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda K^+ \pi^0$ and $\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda K^+ \eta$ using data recorded with the Belle and Belle II detectors at the KEKB and SuperKEKB $e^+ e^-$ colliders. The data samples correspond to integrated luminosities of 980$\,\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ and 570$\,\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$, respectively. We measure their branching-fraction ratios relative to the Cabibbo-favored decay $\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda \pi^+ \pi^0$ as $\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda K^+ \pi^0)/\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda \pi^+ \pi^0)=(1.82 \pm 0.07 \pm 0.07)\%$, with significantly improved precision compared to the previous measurement, and $\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda K^+ \eta)/\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda \pi^+ \pi^0)=(8.73 \pm 0.35 \pm 0.43)\times10^{-3}$, corresponding to the first observation of the $\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda K^+ \eta$ decay with a statistical significance exceeding 10 standard deviations. The first and second uncertainties are statistical and systematic, respectively. Using the world-average value of the branching fraction for $\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda \pi^+ \pi^0$, we obtain the branching fractions $\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda K^+ \pi^0)=(1.29\pm 0.05\pm 0.05\pm 0.06)\times10^{-3}$ and $\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda K^+ \eta)=(6.20\pm 0.25\pm 0.31\pm 0.30)\times10^{-4}$, where the third uncertainty arises from the uncertainty in the world-average value of $\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda \pi^+ \pi^0)$. The measured $\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+\to\Lambda K^+\eta)$ is significantly larger than predictions based on $SU(3)$ flavor symmetry.

</details>


### [5] [A superconducting LC resonator in a multi-tesla magnetic field for tabletop kHz gravitational-wave detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31430)
*D. Ahn,C. Braggio,G. Carugno,D. D'Agostino,R. Di Vora,P. Falferi,A. Ortolan,G. Ruoso,M. L. Ruggiero*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: 本文提出一种基于超导LC谐振器在静态磁场中探测千赫兹频段引力波的新概念，利用引力波在磁场中诱导的振荡磁扰动，通过超导拾取电路和SQUID/量子极限放大器链读出磁通信号；建立了三环耦合电路模型，推导了包含热噪声、SQUID反作用及测量不精确性的等效应变噪声谱；原型实验实现Q≈5×10⁵，目标Q=10⁷；在理想量子极限SQUID噪声下，预计应变灵敏度达~10⁻²¹ Hz⁻¹/²（1–20 kHz内100 Hz带宽），具备紧凑、可调谐特性，可补充干涉仪探测并激发对高频天体物理源的研究。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 拓展引力波探测频段至kHz范围（1–20 kHz），弥补当前地面激光干涉仪（如LIGO）在该频段灵敏度不足的空白，并探索高频天体物理源（如中子星非轴对称振荡、相变引力波等）；发展紧凑、可调谐、非干涉式替代技术。

Method: 基于广义相对论中引力波与电磁场耦合的线性响应理论，在长波近似下建模引力波在静态磁场B₀中诱导的等效振荡磁扰动δB ∝ h₊,× B₀；构建超导三环耦合电路模型（含主LC谐振器、拾取线圈与SQUID输入线圈），严格推导系统哈密顿量与输入-输出关系；完整计入热噪声（谐振器本征热涨落）、SQUID量子反作用噪声（由虚部阻抗引入的辐射压力类项）与测量不精确噪声（SQUID通量噪声折算至应变）；采用标准量子极限（SQL）框架量化总等效应变噪声谱Sₕ(f)。

Result: 原型器件实测品质因数Q ≈ 5 × 10⁵；理论预测在Q = 10⁷、B₀ ~ several Tesla、优化耦合因子及量子极限SQUID读出条件下，应变噪声谱密度达Sₕ^{1/2}(f) ~ 10⁻²¹ Hz⁻¹/₂（在1–20 kHz内100 Hz分析带宽）；噪声预算表明主导贡献为SQUID测量不精确噪声与热噪声，反作用噪声在SQL下可控；增大高场有效体积与提升量子极限读出效率是主要增敏路径。

Conclusion: 超导LC磁感应型引力波探测器是一种物理上自洽、技术上可行的kHz频段新方案；其紧凑性、频率可调性（通过调节L或C）及与现有超导量子测量平台兼容性，使其成为地面干涉仪的重要互补手段；该工作为开发下一代高频引力波探测器提供了理论框架与关键实验验证。

Abstract: We present a concept for a superconducting inductance--capacitance (LC) resonator immersed in a magnetic field for gravitational-wave detection in the kHz band. In the long-wavelength limit, a gravitational wave in a static magnetic field induces an oscillating magnetic-field perturbation proportional to the static magnetic field applied to the resonator and to the gravitational-wave strain. We show that a superconducting pickup circuit can convert this perturbation into a flux signal read out by a superconducting quantum interference device (SQUID)-based or quantum-limited amplifier chain. Inspired by resonant-mass detectors, we develop a three-loop coupled-circuit model and derive the equivalent strain-noise spectrum, including thermal noise, SQUID backaction, and measurement imprecision. Prototype measurements demonstrate a quality factor of $Q\simeq 5\times 10^5$, while $Q=10^7$ is considered as a future development target. Under the assumed flux coupling and an idealized quantum-limited SQUID noise model, optimized circuits yield projected strain sensitivities of order $10^{-21}\,\mathrm{Hz}^{-1/2}$ over $100\,\mathrm{Hz}$ bands in the $1$--$20\,\mathrm{kHz}$ range. Increasing the active high-field volume and improving quantum-limited readout could enable further sensitivity gains. This compact, tunable detector concept could complement interferometric gravitational-wave searches and motivate investigations of high-frequency astrophysical sources.

</details>

### [6] [Alexander Torsion and Floer Comparisons in the BV-BFV Formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30192)
*Nima Moshayedi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: 本文比较了Abelian BF理论的相对挠率与结Floer同调的Alexander分次欧拉示性数，通过环面边界条件将归一化相对挠率与Alexander多项式联系起来；在尖锐的亏格界下，单个素循环挠率值可恢复其全部系数，并兼容连通和拼接与分支覆盖公式；进一步计算了界面收缩及标量比较的障碍，包括零次环面代数双扇区约化的配对缺陷；构造了六生成元dg分解，实现了保持导出配对的完美模范畴等价，并给出经典一维BV-BFV实现；在非共振多项式量子BFV扇区（特征零、ℏ可逆）中，态与Weyl可观测量均退化为真空场，而Hochschild不变量阻碍了其与零次环面代数的Morita等价；最终指出尚未建立完整的几何BV-BFV/Floer等价。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 建立Abelian BF理论与 knot Floer homology 之间的精细不变量对应，尤其在Alexander多项式与相对挠率之间构建可计算、可推广的桥梁，并探索其在BV-BFV框架下的几何实现与范畴等价性。

The tidal response is also investigated, and an inverse relation between the estimated mass and dimensionless tidal deformability is obtained. The calculated configurations span \(2.88\lesssim M_{\rm Est}/M_\odot\lesssim3.23\) and yield \(22.55\lesssim\Lambda\lesssim68.38\). A Kerr black hole with spin $a\simeq0.3$--$0.4$ reproduces the same pairs, so the inferred deformation most likely absorbs the spin neglected by the static metric. Our results therefore describe degeneracies among orbital-frequency deformations; they are not a measurement of an electric charge or of a cosmological constant.

Abstract: This thesis investigates the perturbative dynamics and phenomenology of compact objects in two approaches to quantum gravity: quadratic gravity and asymptotic safety. Linear perturbations provide a tool to probe the physical properties of spacetimes and their potential observational signatures, including quasinormal modes, black hole shadows, and Hawking radiation. The first part focuses on Einstein-Weyl gravity, where an additional massive spin-2 degree of freedom modifies the dynamics of compact objects. We also investigate universal massive-field tails and the breakdown of the standard eikonal correspondence between quasinormal modes and null geodesics. Particular attention is devoted to veiled singularities, horizonless configurations that satisfy a form of weak cosmic censorship and can mimic black hole shadows. The second part examines regular black holes in asymptotically safe gravity. Using the proper-time flow approach, we construct and investigate a dynamical regular black hole solution arising from gravitational collapse. We analyze its perturbative stability and quasinormal mode spectrum, finding characteristic deviations from Schwarzschild black holes. We explore the phenomenological implications of quantum corrections for shadows and Hawking radiation. While shadow remains compatible with current constraints, the modified evaporation process features lower temperatures and suppressed emission rates, with implications for primordial black holes and their possible contribution to dark matter. Overall, this work demonstrates how the perturbative dynamics of compact objects can connect quantum gravity models to potentially observable astrophysical phenomena.

Abstract: We report a matrix-free numerical pipeline for semiclassical false-vacuum decay in a four-component global-O(4) scalar theory in four Euclidean dimensions. For the benchmark potential, the bounce action is B = 145.203947 and the tail residual is 0.281955%. On an L = 26, h = 0.8 lattice, the relaxed field achieves an RMS EOM residual of 4.43 x 10^-12; its low spectrum contains one negative radial mode, four lattice translations, and three near-zero internal modes. The 16-probe bare SLQ trace-log is -2079.9042 +/- 6.4504. Under the subtraction prescription, the scalar partial-wave calculation returns a primed trace-log of 40.9324 at mu = mF = sqrt(0.4), giving a determinant factor of 1.2931 x 10^-9. We show the leading rho^8 potential is not closed under its scalar one-loop divergence: local rho^10 and rho^12 counterterms are required, leaving finite coefficients unmatched; hence this is a prescription-level result, not a complete EFT prediction. The translation-normalized scalar expression is 5.9974 x 10^-70 in lattice energy units to the fourth power, omitting the internal-orientation Jacobian. We summarize tested SU(2) operator and pole-algebra infrastructure, identifying finite full-gauge matching and controlled limits as open work.

We prove that with gauging and active Cartan flux, the complex scalar bilinear vanishes identically. We find that BPS-flow solutions realize this setting. With supersymmetry, we isolate a single independent component of the 3-form equation across an exhaustive four-class partition for both theories within this setup. Abstract: We develop a simple Lorentz-covariant construction of the Born--Infeld-type cubic interactions $(s, s_1, s_2)$ of massless higher-spin fields with $s + s_1 + s_2$ derivatives in four-dimensiona flat space. In contrast to the famous Berends--Burgers--van Dam currents, these currents are constructed from higher-spin Weyl tensors of a single chirality, schematically $J \sim \partial C \partial C$. Starting with a general ansatz, we derive recurrence relations fixing the current coefficients, identify the unique non-trivial conserved current after eliminating trivial (fake) vertices, and show that in the range $s \le s_1 + s_2$ the interaction admits a manifestly gauge-invariant representation in terms of higher-spin Weyl tensors. In the $\mathcal{N}=1$ case, we obtain the general conserved integer superspin $s$ supercurrent with manifest supersymmetry, which is bilinear in the Weyl supertensors of superspins $Y_1$ and $Y_2$. For identical superfields, $Y_1 = Y_2$, this supercurrent vanishes for odd $s$ and is in agreement with earlier results by Gates and Koutrolikos. In the $\mathcal{N}=2$ case, using harmonic superspace, we construct the principal higher-spin supercurrent whose coefficients are uniquely fixed by the conservation equations. In contrast to the $\mathcal{N}=0,1$ cases, for identical $\mathcal{N}=2$ superfields, the corresponding vertex does not vanish. The results provide a systematic framework for Born--Infeld-type higher-spin interactions and their supersymmetric extensions.

Abstract: The completeness hypothesis states that quantum gravity realizes every representation allowed by its gauge group. Previous S-matrix arguments used the graviton pole to constrain exchanged representations, including a proof of completeness by imposing an additional high-energy bound on crossing-odd amplitudes. We investigate what follows without this assumption, using standard twice-subtracted dispersion relations, positivity, and crossing symmetry. For external massless SU(2) isospin $J$, we prove ``half-completeness'': either massive exchanges include every isospin $j\le J$, or at least one $j>J$ is required. At $J=1$, we construct an explicit amplitude with only $j=0,1$ exchanges that satisfies these conditions but violates the crossing-odd bound. This establishes that the additional high-energy assumption does not follow from the standard framework. For $J\ge2$, however, we show that lower-only spectra cannot be realized within a class of string-inspired Euler amplitudes, leaving their existence an open question. More generally, for scalar matter transforming under any compact internal symmetry group, we prove that massive singlets of unbounded Lorentz spin must be exchanged.

Abstract: Meson-isospectral pair rotations can closely reproduce low-lying baryon energies while producing a substantial connected action under their consistent three-body embedding. Starting from an independently calibrated S-wave meson model, we compare ground-mass fits, joint ground-level and gap fits, and direct minimization of the connected remainder. With eighteen shared coefficients, a four-packet joint fit reproduces the nine calibration ground masses and first gaps with RMS errors of $\JointGroundTrain$ and $\JointGapTrain$ MeV, respectively. The excluded ground masses and gaps have RMS errors of $10.7$ and $8.1$ MeV in the same enlarged radial space. Its connected action on the eighteen calibrated inputs is $\JointActionCal$ times the unrotated source action. Eigenvalue agreement and removal of the connected interaction thus impose different tests. Separately optimizing the action with five packets gives a normalized residual of $\ShortActionAll$ for an intermediate-distance fifth packet; a long-distance extension also leaves a substantial remainder. An eliminated-state construction realizes the central color--color structure of an intermediate-configuration mechanism together with correlated pair dressing. These results favor a connected operator basis with three fitted strengths as an economical description within the tested pair family, and quantify its relation to the representation freedom of equivalent few-body Hamiltonians.

Abstract: We show that $^{229}$Th nuclear clocks can probe departures from the Peccei-Quinn (PQ) solution to the strong CP problem. Explicit PQ breaking or additional CP-violating interactions can shift the minimum of the axion potential and generate CP-violating couplings to matter, inducing a clock response linear in the axion field. We investigate explicit PQ breaking and quark chromo-electric dipole operators as representative realizations of these \textit{imperfect axion} scenarios, consistent with electric dipole moment constraints. For axion dark matter, the linear signal oscillates at the Compton frequency and can dominate the quadratic response. Even without axion dark matter, the Earth can source a static axion field, enabling fifth-force searches with nuclear clocks.

TL;DR: 本文通过闭合测试框架，比较了高斯过程、神经网络集成和经验神经正切核近似等机器学习方法在非单态三重态部分子分布函数（PDF）重构中的不确定性估计能力，强调了实验协方差、函数先验及核评估时机对不确定性质量的关键影响。

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 在物理逆问题中，尤其是PDF确定中，重建函数的不确定性估计与中心值同等重要；现有ML方法缺乏对不确定性校准的系统性验证。

Abstract: Machine-learning methods are increasingly used to solve inverse problems in physics, where a faithful estimate of the uncertainty on the reconstructed function is as important as its central value. We address this question in the simplest instance of a parton distribution function (PDF) determination: the reconstruction of the non-singlet triplet distribution $T_3(x,Q_0^2)$ from deep-inelastic scattering data. Within a closure-test framework, in which the underlying PDF is known and the pseudo-data carry correlated experimental uncertainties, we compare Gaussian processes with different kernels, neural-network ensembles trained with different loss functions, and empirical neural tangent kernel approximations. We find that methods yielding comparable central reconstructions can produce markedly different uncertainties, and we identify the ingredients that control them: a likelihood that includes the full experimental covariance, the functional prior encoded in the kernel or in the endpoint preprocessing, and, for tangent-kernel approximations, the stage of training at which the kernel is evaluated. A normalised-bias diagnostic shows that calibration depends on the correlations of the predictive covariance, not only on its pointwise width. Our results illustrate why closure tests and covariance-aware diagnostics are essential to validate uncertainties in ML-based approaches to inverse problems.

</details>

Abstract: We investigate the prospects for improving the heavy neutral lepton (HNL) searches at the far-forward LHC experiments FASER and FASER$\nu$, as well as their proposed HL-LHC successors, FASER2 and FASER$\nu$2, using new HNL production mechanisms. We consider scenarios in which HNLs couple directly to a new $U(1)$ gauge boson and decay into visible Standard Model final states through mixing with active neutrinos. Focusing on the $U(1)_{B-L}$, $U(1)_{B-3L_\tau}$, and $U(1)_B$ gauge extensions as benchmark models, we study HNL production through proton bremsstrahlung and Drell--Yan processes, which probe complementary mediator-mass regimes. We analyze several visible decay channels, including charged leptons, neutral mesons, and other hadrons, and evaluate the projected sensitivities for different neutrino-flavor mixing patterns. We find that these new production mechanisms that differ qualitatively from the conventional picture in which both production and decay are controlled by active-sterile mixing enable the far-forward LHC program to probe previously unexplored regions of HNL parameter space, with the first-ever demonstration of FASER$\nu$ having sensitivity to HNLs and FASER2 and FASER$\nu$2 offering substantially enhanced sensitivity during the HL-LHC era, even reaching the canonical seesaw prediction for all three lepton flavors. Our results demonstrate that new gauge interactions can substantially enhance HNL production independently of the active-sterile mixing that governs their visible decays, opening complementary opportunities to explore HNLs at the forward physics facilities.

Abstract: Neural simulation-based inference has been shown to extract significantly more information from LHC data than histogram-based analyses. So far, it has mostly been applied to couplings, for which the polynomial structure of the scattering amplitude can be exploited. Masses and widths instead move and deform the signal in phase space, and learning their likelihood requires many event samples covering the continuous theory parameter space. We show how sequential simulation-based inference can address this by iteratively concentrating the simulations where the observed data points. Using a parton-level example with an exact reference likelihood and reconstruction-level analyses of $A\to ZH\to \ell\ell b\bar b$ and $Y\to HH\to (t\bar t)(t\bar t)$, we demonstrate that sequential training produces significantly more accurate and better calibrated likelihoods than single round training with the same amount of training events.

</details>

Abstract: We provide a fully quantum-mechanical description of the continuous measurement of wavelike dark matter. Our focus is cavity haloscopes, although our results inform a broad range of axion searches. We extend the input-output formalism by treating the axion as a quantum field, finding how its quantum state is imprinted onto higher-order moments of observables of the outgoing transmission-line modes. We further develop a novel measurement-record formalism, which computes the joint probability distribution of all measurement outcomes and is well suited to studying the axion in the time domain. With it, we compute the exact axion-induced number distribution for the first time, demonstrating that it is not Poisson. We argue that virialized axion dark matter is expected to be in a Gaussian quantum state, and show that number or quadrature measurements can distinguish this from the predictions of a coherent state or even a Bose-Einstein condensate. Finally, we show that intrinsically quantum effects, such as those from Fock or squeezed axion states, are far too weak to detect.

Abstract: We study Neutrino Transition Polarizability, where a light pseudoscalar $\phi$ couples active neutrinos to heavy neutral leptons $N$ and interacts with two photons. For $m_N>m_\phi$, the decay $N\to\nu\phi$ can greatly shorten the heavy neutrino lifetime, while $\phi\to\gamma\gamma$ produces a displaced photon pair. This changes the relevant search strategies, favoring production and decay inside detectors. We discuss existing constraints and estimate the sensitivity of displaced photon searches at DUNE, Belle II, LHCb and FCC-ee, highlighting the complementarity of neutrino scattering and meson and electroweak boson decays.

Abstract: Ultralight scalars that vary dark-matter masses require protection from quantum corrections. Cyclic $\mathbb{Z}_N$ symmetries provide this protection but also suppress variation of the mean mass of equally populated partners. Unsuppressed variation typically requires asymmetric reheating through symmetry-breaking couplings that threaten radiative control. Abstract: Axion-like particles (ALPs) can be copiously produced in the hot and dense environment of a core-collapse supernova. If their mass is within an order of magnitude of the supernova temperature, a non-negligible fraction of them will remain gravitationally bound to the neutron star remnant. This basin of ALPs can then decay to photons over the lifetime of the neutron star, thereby reheating it. Using the measured luminosities of several neutron stars, we close multiple windows in ALP parameter space while also satisfying the SN 1987A cooling constraint on the ALP-nucleon coupling. In particular, using the 35000 K upper limit on the temperature of the coldest neutron star observed, PSR J2144$-$3933, we derive the strongest upper limits on the ALP-photon coupling for 1$-$400 MeV ALP masses. Ceilings on these limits arising from ALP decays occurring within the neutron star lifetime may be overcome by using younger neutron stars such as the one in Cas A, probing couplings orders of magnitude higher. Observations of a $2000 \, {\rm K}$-hot neutron star in infrared observatories such as the James Webb Space Telescope could reach ALP-photon couplings three orders lower and ALP masses one order lower than our current limits.

Abstract: Long-range interactions play an important role in dark-matter phenomenology, affecting the relic abundance, indirect-detection signals, and structure formation on galactic scales. Reliable predictions often require nonperturbative solutions of the Schrödinger equation over broad parameter ranges and many partial waves. We present a numerical framework based on the variable-phase method, which recasts the radial Schrödinger equation as a system of first-order equations and allows both regular and irregular solutions to be computed efficiently. The former determine phase shifts and Sommerfeld factors, while the latter provide additional information relevant for the self-consistent unitarization of contact-like inelastic interactions. We discuss the numerical implementation, integration-domain selection, partial-wave convergence, and comparisons with analytic limits. We release the code together with tables for the Yukawa potential containing phase shifts, Sommerfeld factors, elastic and angularly weighted cross sections, and the irregular-solution information relevant for unitarization. These resources facilitate dark-matter studies without requiring repeated computationally intensive scans.
